        This document presents Swirl-String Theory (SST) as a structured canonical synthesis rather than as a claim that every phenomenological sector has already been derived to theorem level. The text records the impedance--resonance and free-to-bound-mode origin of the programme; separates the unfixed quasi-static response coefficient from its legacy numerical reference while retaining the characteristic swirl speed, Compton anchoring, and horn/circulation scale; assigns a minimal relational link-field action as the microscopic research candidate beneath the continuum torsion bridge; records the operational special-relativity postulate that stable vortex matter constructs local spacetime with clocks, rods, and luminal R-phase torsion pulses; adds a conditional action--phase mass--shell route in which the internal carrier phase slows by the Lorentz factor without requiring translational flattening or stretching of the knot; and organizes delay selection, atomic bridges, spectroscopy, knot-geometry certification, KAM-caged dynamical stability, relational time, and topology-based mass within one explicitly stratified framework. Throughout, historical provenance, calibrated identities, SST-internal derivations, predictions, and conjectural extensions are kept distinct so that the canon remains internally coherent, externally comparable, and open to falsification. Version~0.8.33 additionally fixes the differential-form degree rules for any covariant vortex-worldsheet description, introduces a guarded parity-sensitive response interface, and replaces inherited gauge-emergence assertions by an explicit algebra, representation, anomaly, and phenomenology certification ladder.
    \end{abstract}

\section*{Author's Origin Note: From Impedance and Resonance to Vorticity}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Author's Origin Note: From Impedance and Resonance to Vorticity}
\label{sec:authors_origin_note}

Swirl--String Theory did not begin as a fluid-mechanical model built from a
preselected set of hydrodynamic constants. Its first written development arose
from a different question: whether the apparent constants and discontinuities
of atomic excitation could be understood as consequences of a classical,
local, resonant interaction. The earliest notebooks from 2012 and the first
synthesis document from July 2013 investigated stored field energy,
capacitance, wavelength, oscillator stiffness, internal rotation, and the
transition between a freely propagating excitation and a temporarily or
permanently bound rotating configuration.

The guiding intuition was that the quantum jump should not be treated as a
primitive discontinuity without mechanism. It should instead be examined as a
finite dynamical transfer between a free field mode and a bound material mode.
The early language was electrical and mechanical: capacitance, dielectric
response, oscillation, characteristic speed, force, and impedance. The later
language of circulation, vorticity, vortex tubes, toroidal geometry, knots, and
relative vortex clocks was developed in order to provide a more coherent
dynamical substrate for that earlier program.

This historical order matters. The present Canon must not imply that the
current symbols $\Gamma_0$, $\rhoF$, and $\rc$ were the original ontological
starting point. They are candidate parameters of a later continuum
representation. The older and more persistent commitment is structural:
freely propagating radiation and bound matter are different dynamical regimes
of one underlying medium, and their coupling is controlled by the response and
impedance of that medium.

The historical documents are not treated as evidence that the proposed physics
is correct. They are primary provenance records. Their role is to preserve the
origin of the research questions, prevent later formulations from erasing the
original logic, and identify which modern results must be derived independently
rather than reconstructed algebraically from known constants.

\begin{quote}
\small
\textbf{Canonical origin statement.}
SST originated as an impedance--resonance attempt to explain atomic excitation,
the proportionality between energy and frequency, the emergence of mass from a
bound rotating state, and the relation between internal material speed and the
free propagation speed. Vorticity and knot topology are later dynamical
languages introduced to make that program local, conservative, geometric, and
falsifiable.
\end{quote}

\section{Genesis, Foundational Commitments, and Epistemic Architecture}
\label{sec:genesis_epistemic_architecture}

\subsection{Purpose and canonical scope}
\label{subsec:genesis_scope}

This section records three distinct layers that must not be conflated:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{historical origin}: what the notebooks and the 2013
    proto-Canon actually proposed;
    \item \textbf{modern SST translation}: how those ideas are now expressed
    using vortex, link-field, topological, and operational-spacetime language;
    \item \textbf{current epistemic status}: which statements are definitions,
    hypotheses, calibrated bridges, derived consequences, predictions, or
    falsifiers.
\end{enumerate}

No historical claim becomes canonical merely because it appeared early. No
modern equation may be projected backward into the notebooks as if it had been
explicitly present there. Conversely, a modern reformulation should not erase
the earlier physical question that motivated it.

\subsection{Primary provenance record}
\label{subsec:primary_provenance_record}

The present origin layer is anchored by the following source classes:
\begin{description}
    \item[Notebook 2012.] The earliest surviving page-level record of rotating
    charge, free versus bound wave structures, toroidal geometry, Lorentz
    transformations, oscillator-speed constructions, explicit circulation,
    vorticity, and the capacitance path.

    \item[\texttt{DOC2013-CANON-001}.] \emph{Een hypothese over de constantes in
    de kwantum mechanica, verklaard met de klassieke logica}, July 2013. This is
    the first surviving attempt to turn the notebook ideas into a single
    explanatory document. It explicitly introduced an excitation or internal
    circumferential speed, treated mass as a rotating state, interpreted the
    fine-structure constant as a speed ratio, and developed a capacitance-based
    route to the Planck relation.

    \item[Notebook 2013--2014.] Subsequent geometric and algebraic development,
    including toroidal field structures, relative rotation, clock ideas,
    impedance reasoning, and attempts to interpret the earlier numerical
    closures.

    \item[Modern SST Canon.] The current hydrodynamic and topological
    architecture, including link-field dynamics, operational spacetime, and
    falsification rules.

    \item[Modern computational provenance.] The 2026 KnotPlot-to-Ridgerunner
    pipeline, geometry sidecars, seed-selection records, certified and candidate
    knot exports, and downstream SSTcore/VortexLab audits. These records test the
    translation from a closed-mode idea to resolved finite-thickness geometry.
\end{description}

Stable notebook identifiers such as \texttt{NB2012-P070} and
\texttt{NB2013-P019} shall be used in the provenance appendix. Their purpose is
traceability, not authority.

\subsection{Historical development of the core idea}
\label{subsec:historical_development_core}

The source record supports the following developmental sequence:
\begin{align}
    &\text{stored field energy and capacitance}
    \nonumber\\[-0.2em]
    &\quad\longrightarrow
    \text{resonant photon--matter interaction}
    \nonumber\\[-0.2em]
    &\quad\longrightarrow
    \text{internal circumferential or excitation speed}
    \nonumber\\[-0.2em]
    &\quad\longrightarrow
    \text{mass as a bound rotating state}
    \nonumber\\[-0.2em]
    &\quad\longrightarrow
    \text{relative time and spatial deformation of rotating matter}
    \nonumber\\[-0.2em]
    &\quad\longrightarrow
    \text{circulation, vorticity, toroidal cores, and vortex knots}
    \nonumber\\[-0.2em]
    &\quad\longrightarrow
    \text{link-field propagation and operational spacetime}.
    \label{eq:historical_sst_development_chain}
\end{align}

The 2013 proto-Canon already contained an important epistemic caution. After
rewriting the Planck relation from known electromagnetic quantities and the new
excitation speed, it described the result as, in effect, a calculation trick
unless the same structure could be recovered through another independent
combination. That caution is retained here as a canonical methodological rule:
algebraic closure is not yet dynamical explanation.

\subsection{Modern computational provenance: from KnotPlot seeds to certified finite-thickness representatives}
\label{subsec:genesis_knotplot_ridgerunner_provenance}

The 2012--2014 source record proposed closed recurrent field structures before
SST possessed a controlled finite-thickness knot geometry.  The modern
KnotPlot-to-Ridgerunner project is the first systematic attempt to resolve that
old bound-mode intuition as an auditable embedded tube rather than as a sketch,
a knot diagram, or a scalar topological label.  Its role in the Genesis record
is therefore methodological: it connects the early claim ``matter is a closed
self-maintaining mode'' to a reproducible geometry pipeline.  It does not by
itself establish that physical particles are ideal knots.

\paragraph{Three distinct computational roles.}
The project separates three layers which must remain distinct:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{KnotPlot exploration.} KnotPlot creates and relaxes isotopic
    candidate centerlines and supplies checkpoint families.  It is a seed and
    topology-exploration tool, not a final smooth-thickness certificate.

    \item \textbf{Ridgerunner certification.} Ridgerunner minimizes polygonal
    length subject to explicit finite-thickness constraints and reports a
    first-order constrained-gradient balance through active struts and kinks
    \cite{AshtonCantarellaPiatekRawdon2011}.

    \item \textbf{SSTcore/VortexLab use.} A certified or candidate centerline is
    converted into a separately versioned, uniformly arc-length sampled copy
    for Biot--Savart, reach, clock, parity, or spectroscopy diagnostics.  The
    original Ridgerunner polish remains the audit geometry.
\end{enumerate}
A successful result in one layer is not silently promoted to the next.

\paragraph{Orthodox finite-thickness core.}
For a smooth embedded centerline $\gamma$, radius-convention thickness is
\begin{align}
    \operatorname{Thi}_{\rm rad}(\gamma)
    =
    \min\!\left(
        \inf_s\frac{1}{\kappa(s)},
        \frac12d_{\rm dcsd}(\gamma)
    \right),
    \label{eq:genesis_knotplot_thickness_split}
\end{align}
where $d_{\rm dcsd}$ is the minimum doubly-critical self-distance.  Thus a
resolved tube is limited either by local curvature or by nonlocal self-contact.
In a polygonal approximation these active constraints become the kink and
strut sets.  At constrained criticality their nonnegative multipliers balance
the length or declared SST energy gradient,
\begin{align}
    -\nabla E
    +
    \sum_{(p,q)\in\operatorname{Strut}(V)}
        \lambda_{pq}\nabla\!\left(\frac{d(p,q)}{2}\right)
    +
    \sum_{i\in\operatorname{Kink}(V)}
        \mu_i\nabla\operatorname{MinRad}_i
    =0,
    \qquad
    \lambda_{pq},\mu_i\ge0.
    \label{eq:genesis_knotplot_contact_balance}
\end{align}
Ropelength minimizers exist in every tame knot type and are $C^{1,1}$, but they
need not be $C^2$ or unique \cite{CantarellaKusnerSullivan2002}.  Therefore knot
type alone does not select one unique physical centerline, and a scalar
ropelength cannot determine every shape-sensitive SST observable.

\paragraph{Project conclusion 1: ``ideal'' is a claim to be certified.}
A file labelled \emph{ideal}, a visually tight KnotPlot configuration, a
Fourier atlas entry, or a small scalar ropelength excess is not by itself a
geometric certificate.  The project found that the same knot may pass a length
comparison while still lacking strict residual convergence, resolution
convergence, smooth-thickness verification, or a stable contact skeleton.

\begin{definition}[Geometry certification ladder]
\label{def:geometry_certification_ladder}
Every knot geometry used in a downstream SST claim carries exactly one of the
graded statuses
\begin{equation}
    \begin{gathered}
        \texttt{seed}<\texttt{relaxed candidate}<\texttt{near-ideal candidate},\\
        \texttt{near-ideal candidate}<\texttt{strict near-ideal}
        <\texttt{certified smooth upper bound},
    \end{gathered}
    \label{eq:genesis_knotplot_status_ladder}
\end{equation}
determined solely by the preregistered gate profile of the release in which the
geometry was produced.
\end{definition}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{[CANON RULE].}
No SST manuscript, benchmark, or numerical result may cite a geometry above its
certified status, silently promote it between statuses, or weaken a gate profile
after inspecting a physical output.  Downstream claims must expose the geometry
source, topology certificate, thickness convention, resolution, constrained
residual, contact map, and certified error envelope.
\end{quote}
No software or database name substitutes for these gates.

\paragraph{Project conclusion 2: topology and geometry require independent gates.}
The automatic pipeline records a topology sidecar for every checkpoint,
including at least a safety flag, component count, vertices per component, and
pairwise linking matrix where applicable.  A geometrically plausible seed with
missing or inconsistent topology metadata receives the status
\texttt{geometry-qualified-topology-unverified}; automatic tightening stops
rather than guessing.  The specific trial number selected in a run is
provenance metadata, not a canonical physical fact.

\paragraph{Project conclusion 3: checkpoint selection must not be ``take the latest''.}
KnotPlot checkpoint quality is not assumed to improve monotonically.  The
project therefore uses signed fractional length gain, per-component flatness,
and a finite-thickness proxy
\begin{align}
    D_{\rm proxy}
    &=
    \min\!\left(2R_{\min},d_{\rm self}^{\rm nonlocal},d_{\rm inter}\right),
    \\
    \mathcal R_{\rm proxy}
    &=
    \frac{L}{D_{\rm proxy}},
    \label{eq:genesis_knotplot_seed_proxy}
\end{align}
rather than raw length alone.  A signed plateau requires nonnegative but small
improvements over successive checkpoints; a length regression is never called
a plateau.  If no plateau is found within the preregistered scan range, the
pipeline may retain a best-so-far seed only with an explicit
\texttt{best-so-far-no-plateau} warning.

\paragraph{Project conclusion 4: radius and diameter conventions must be explicit.}
The Brian Gilbert Fourier database records $D=1$ and, for the benchmark entries,
\begin{align}
    L(3{:}1{:}1)&=16.371637,
    &
    L(4{:}1{:}1)&=21.043322.
    \label{eq:genesis_gilbert_length_benchmarks}
\end{align}
Under the radius-thickness convention used in the SST ropelength sections,
these correspond to
\begin{align}
    \operatorname{Rop}_{\rm rad}(3_1)&=32.743274,
    &
    \operatorname{Rop}_{\rm rad}(4_1)&=42.086644.
    \label{eq:genesis_gilbert_rop_benchmarks}
\end{align}
Any comparison that omits the factor-of-two convention is rejected before
physical interpretation.

\paragraph{Project conclusion 5: uniform sampling and physical relaxation are different operations.}
The raw tightened polygon is retained unchanged for geometry audit.  A separate
copy is uniformly resampled in arc length for downstream field solvers.  The
uniform copy improves quadrature and removes mesh-density bias; it does not
improve the physical optimum and does not erase the original residual or
contact data.  For strict resolution convergence, the current best Ridgerunner
polish is used as the common parent, independently resampled to $N=600$ and
$N=1200$, and each branch is re-relaxed and re-certified.  The convergence
study does not restart KnotPlot and does not treat the read-only VortexLab
$N=300$ export as a new canonical seed.

\paragraph{Current strict near-ideal profile.}
The present SST project profile requires, for a strict near-ideal label,
\begin{align}
    \chi_{\rm KKT}&\le5\times10^{-3},
    &
    \epsilon_{\rm Rop}&\le5\times10^{-4},
    \\
    \epsilon_{\rm multistart}&\le2\times10^{-4},
    &
    \epsilon_{600\rightarrow1200}&\le2\times10^{-4},
    \\
    \frac{\ell_{\max}}{\ell_{\min}}&\le1.02,
    &
    \operatorname{CV}(\ell)&\le5\times10^{-3}.
    \label{eq:genesis_strict_near_ideal_profile}
\end{align}
These are project acceptance thresholds, not mathematical constants of nature.
They may be tightened in later releases, but they must not be weakened after
seeing a desired physical output.

\paragraph{Trefoil snapshot and status discipline.}
One dated $N=300$ project snapshot derived from a Ridgerunner polish reports
$L=16.3763431828$ at $D=1$, corresponding to
$\operatorname{Rop}_{\rm rad}=32.7526863657$.  Relative to the Gilbert reference
in Equation~\eqref{eq:genesis_gilbert_rop_benchmarks}, the excess is about
$2.87\times10^{-4}$, while the uniform export has edge ratio approximately
$1.000693$ and edge-length coefficient of variation approximately
$8.24\times10^{-5}$.  It passes topology, ropelength, uniform-mesh, and the
looser $\chi_{\rm KKT}\le10^{-2}$ candidate gate, but fails the strict residual
gate and has not yet completed the $600\rightarrow1200$ convergence test.
Its correct status is therefore \texttt{near-ideal-candidate}, not
\texttt{strict-near-ideal}.  This example is deliberately retained because it
shows that excellent scalar length agreement is not equivalent to complete
certification.

\paragraph{Project conclusion 6: the contact skeleton is the physically richer output.}
For SST, the active set
\begin{align}
    \mathcal C_K
    =
    \left(
        \operatorname{Strut}(V),
        \operatorname{Kink}(V),
        \{\lambda_{pq}\},
        \{\mu_i\},
        \operatorname{cond}A_F
    \right)
    \label{eq:genesis_contact_skeleton}
\end{align}
contains more physical information than the scalar ropelength.  It identifies
where the resolved tube is compression-limited, where curvature saturates, how
loads are distributed, and whether the constraint system is well conditioned.
Ropelength convergence without contact-measure convergence is insufficient for
a shape-sensitive mass, magnetic, clock, or impedance claim.

\paragraph{Connection to the rediscovered impedance path.}
The KnotPlot/Ridgerunner project supplies a candidate geometry and contact
skeleton for the \emph{bound} side of the free--bound transition.  It does not
supply the free link-field impedance $Z_\ell$, the transition action
$\mathcal J_0$, or the source normalization $Q_0$.  A future jump impedance
$Z_{\mathrm J}$ may depend on the certified knot domain and its linear response,
but it cannot be identified with ropelength alone.  The non-circular program
therefore requires the chain
\begin{equation}
    \begin{aligned}
        \text{certified finite-thickness geometry}
        &\longrightarrow \text{bound-mode response operator},\\
        \text{bound-mode response operator}
        &\longrightarrow Z_{\mathrm J}(\omega)
        \longrightarrow \text{comparison with }Z_\ell(\omega).
    \end{aligned}
    \label{eq:genesis_bound_impedance_chain}
\end{equation}
with all geometry errors propagated into the final coupling uncertainty.

\paragraph{Canonical status.}
The orthodox thickness, reach, ropelength, and constrained-criticality results
belong in the MAIN Canon.  The specific KnotPlot commands, checkpoint
heuristics, thresholds, trial selections, and current candidate values are
\textbf{[RESEARCH-TRACK / COMPUTATIONAL PROVENANCE]}.  Downstream SST claims
must expose the geometry source, topology certificate, convention, resolution,
residual, contact map, and error envelope used.

\subsection{Regime of applicability of ideal-knot geometry}
\label{subsec:ideal_knot_regime_guard}

\paragraph{Compact reference state.}
\textbf{[CANON / ANSATZ / SCOPE GUARD].}
The ideal trefoil is retained as the maximally thick, ropelength-critical
reference geometry for the compact finite-core electron candidate,
\begin{equation}
    \mathbf X_{\rm compact}
    \simeq
    \mathbf X_{\rm ideal,3_1}.
    \label{eq:ideal_trefoil_compact_reference}
\end{equation}
Equation~\eqref{eq:ideal_trefoil_compact_reference} does not assert that the
ideal trefoil minimizes the radius of the smallest enclosing sphere, minimizes
the complete Euler kinetic-energy functional, or represents every excited
state of the electron.  ``Ideal'' refers to the declared ropelength problem and
its thickness convention; it is not a universal dynamical ground-state label.

\paragraph{Finite-core and slender-filament regimes.}
For a resolved tube with effective core radius $a_{\rm core}$, maximum
centreline curvature $\kappa_{\max}$, and minimum nonlocal centreline separation
$d_{\rm sep}$, define
\begin{equation}
    \epsilon_{\kappa}
    :=a_{\rm core}\kappa_{\max},
    \qquad
    \epsilon_{\rm sep}
    :=\frac{a_{\rm core}}{d_{\rm sep}}.
    \label{eq:ideal_knot_regime_diagnostics}
\end{equation}
The canon distinguishes
\begin{equation}
\begin{array}{lll}
    \text{compact finite-core regime}
    &:&
    \epsilon_{\kappa}=O(1)
    \ \text{or}\ 
    \epsilon_{\rm sep}=O(1),
    \\
    \text{slender-filament regime}
    &:&
    \epsilon_{\kappa}\ll1
    \ \text{and}\ 
    \epsilon_{\rm sep}\ll1.
\end{array}
\label{eq:ideal_knot_regime_split}
\end{equation}
The ideal trefoil belongs primarily to the compact finite-core regime because
its defining constrained optimum can contain active curvature and contact
constraints.  Local-induction, logarithmic slender-body, and perturbative
$B_{\rm eff}\kappa^2$ reductions are not promoted on that state unless
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ideal_knot_regime_diagnostics} and a nonlocal-induction residual
show that a controlled asymptotic regime has actually been reached.

\paragraph{Kirchhoff--Cosserat non-equivalence guard.}
A rod functional may be evaluated as a diagnostic,
\begin{equation}
    E_{\rm rod}^{\rm diag}[\mathbf X,\Omega_3]
    =
    T L[\mathbf X]
    +\frac{B}{2}\int\kappa^2\,ds
    +\frac{C}{2}\int\Omega_3^2\,ds,
    \label{eq:rod_diagnostic_functional}
\end{equation}
where $\Omega_3$ is the material twist strain.  Such an evaluation does not
show that $\mathbf X$ is stationary under this functional and does not supply
canonical SST moduli $B$ and $C$.  An ideal incompressible inviscid fluid has no
material static shear modulus.  Its kinetic energy may nevertheless reduce to
a geometry-dependent first-order vortex Hamiltonian; that reduced Hamiltonian
must not be relabelled as an elastic-rod potential without a separate
hydrodynamic derivation.

Fukumoto's vortex-jet/Kirchhoff correspondence identifies a class of permanent
translating filament shapes with Kirchhoff-rod equilibrium shapes under
specific localized-induction and axial-core-flow assumptions
\cite{Fukumoto2007}.  It is a stationary-shape correspondence, not an
identification of the complete rod and vortex phase spaces.  Consequently
Kirchhoff--Cosserat dynamics, elastic normal modes, and rod constitutive
coefficients are not imported into SST particle dynamics.  Physical elastic
trefoils can also relax to shapes measurably different from perfectly flexible
ideal knots \cite{JohannsEtAl2021}.

\paragraph{Preferred topological fluid bridge.}
For one coherently framed closed thin vortex tube of circulation $\Gamma$, the
hydrodynamic self-linking relation is
\begin{equation}
    \mathcal H
    =
    \Gamma^2 Lk
    =
    \Gamma^2\left(Wr+Tw\right),
    \label{eq:moffatt_ricca_single_tube_helicity}
\end{equation}
under the usual ideal-flow and thin-tube hypotheses
\cite{MoffattRicca1992}.  Equation~\eqref{eq:moffatt_ricca_single_tube_helicity}
provides the canonical topology--fluid bridge.  It carries circulation and
helicity content but introduces no elastic bending modulus.

\paragraph{Use policy.}
The ideal trefoil may be used as a certified compact reference geometry and as
input to finite-core diagnostics.  Kirchhoff--Cosserat quantities may be
reported as comparative geometric diagnostics.  Neither use establishes that
the same geometry is an Euler-energy minimizer, a rod equilibrium, or a
slender-filament solution.  Excited stretched states and the transition from
the compact to the slender regime are retained in the companion research
track, Section~\ref{subsec:rt_compact_to_slender_state}.


\subsection{Foundational physical commitments}
\label{subsec:foundational_physical_commitments}

The following commitments summarize the persistent physical content of the
origin program without promoting unverified historical mechanisms to theorem
status.

\begin{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{One-substrate commitment.}
    Matter, radiation, and operational clocks are organized regimes of one
    underlying medium rather than unrelated primitive ontologies.

    \item \textbf{Free/bound mode distinction.}
    A freely propagating radiation mode and a stable material particle are not
    the same solution. The former is an extended propagating excitation; the
    latter is a closed, recurrent, topologically or dynamically trapped state.

    \item \textbf{Rotational matter commitment.}
    The inertia and clock structure of matter are associated with recurrent
    internal motion. In modern SST this is represented by stable vortex and
    link-field configurations, not by the literal rigid charged sphere of the
    earliest model.

    \item \textbf{Local transition commitment.}
    Absorption and emission are local transfers between a free mode and a bound
    mode. The transition must possess an action, energy flux, coupling law, and
    finite response structure.

    \item \textbf{Impedance commitment.}
    The strength of the free--bound coupling is controlled by the response of
    the propagating link sector relative to the response of the bound
    transition sector.

    \item \textbf{Operational relativity commitment.}
    Stable matter constructs time, length, and simultaneity with its internal
    cycles and exchanged torsion pulses. Relative motion mixes the resulting
    temporal and spatial coordinates while preserving the local torsion cone.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{The original capacitance and transition hypothesis}
\label{subsec:original_capacitance_transition_hypothesis}

\textbf{[ORIGIN / HISTORICAL BRIDGE].}
The early construction combined the classical relations
\begin{align}
    E_{\mathrm{cap}}
    &=
    \frac{Q^2}{2C_{\mathrm{cap}}},
    &
    C_{\mathrm{cap}}
    &=
    \varepsilon\frac{A}{D},
    \label{eq:origin_capacitance_pair}
    \\
    v_{\mathrm{ex}}
    &=
    f\lambda,
    &
    E
    &=
    hf.
    \label{eq:origin_speed_planck_pair}
\end{align}

With the historical geometric ansatz $A=\lambda^2$ and $D=\lambda/2$, the
capacity becomes proportional to $\varepsilon\lambda$. Substitution of
$\lambda=v_{\mathrm{ex}}/f$ then produces a coefficient multiplying $f$ in the
stored-energy expression. Under that specific normalization, the historical
construction may be summarized as
\begin{align}
    h_{\mathrm{bridge}}
    =
    \frac{Q^2}{4\varepsilon v_{\mathrm{ex}}}.
    \label{eq:historical_h_capacitance_bridge}
\end{align}

Equation~\eqref{eq:historical_h_capacitance_bridge} is not a current derivation
of Planck's constant. It depends on an assumed interaction geometry, on the
identification of the relevant charge, on a pre-existing electromagnetic
susceptibility, and on a speed that was itself calibrated from known particle
relations. Moreover, its later impedance form is an exact reparameterization of
this same algebraic construction, not an independent second route. Its canonical
value is that it exposes the original physical question:
\begin{quote}
Can the linear energy--frequency law arise from the static and dynamic response
of a finite transition region rather than being inserted as an unexplained
proportionality?
\end{quote}

\subsection{Impedance form of the origin hypothesis}
\label{subsec:impedance_form_origin_hypothesis}

The capacitance construction can be rewritten more transparently in terms of a
propagation impedance and a transition impedance. This change of variables does
not by itself add independent evidence or close the multiple-route requirement.
For an isotropic lossless
link-field sector, define
\begin{align}
    c_T
    &=
    \frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon_\ell\mu_\ell}},
    &
    Z_\ell
    &=
    \sqrt{\frac{\mu_\ell}{\epsilon_\ell}}
    =
    \frac{1}{\epsilon_\ell c_T}.
    \label{eq:link_speed_impedance_pair}
\end{align}

Here $\epsilon_\ell$ and $\mu_\ell$ are SST link-response coefficients. They
are not assumed to equal the SI vacuum constants. Let $Q_0$ denote a candidate
minimal topological source unit and let $\mathcal J_0$ denote a candidate
minimal transition action. The corresponding transition-impedance scale is
\begin{align}
    Z_{\mathrm J}
    :=
    \frac{\mathcal J_0}{Q_0^2}.
    \label{eq:jump_impedance_definition}
\end{align}

A modern statement of the old fine-structure intuition is then
\begin{align}
    \alpha_{\mathrm{SST}}
    =
    \kappa_Z\frac{Z_\ell}{Z_{\mathrm J}}
    =
    \kappa_Z\frac{Q_0^2Z_\ell}{\mathcal J_0},
    \label{eq:alpha_impedance_candidate}
\end{align}
where $\kappa_Z$ is a dimensionless geometric and normalization factor. The
present electromagnetic bridge corresponds to the target $\kappa_Z=1/2$ under
one conventional normalization, but that value is not to be imposed in the
zero-legacy derivation. It must follow from the transition geometry and source
normalization.

Equation~\eqref{eq:alpha_impedance_candidate} is a \textbf{[RESEARCH
HYPOTHESIS]}. In the historical bridge it is the impedance notation of the same
capacitance identity, so the two forms count as one route. It becomes stronger
than a numerical identity only if $Z_\ell$, $Q_0$, $\mathcal J_0$, and
$\kappa_Z$ are independently obtained from the SST action and are then compared
with a genuinely separate bound-mode, static-source, or transition-action
calculation.

\subsection{Modern link-field translation}
\label{subsec:modern_link_field_translation}

The minimal link action introduced later in this Canon supplies the first
controlled candidate for the propagation side of the origin program. On a
regular network with link spacing $a_\ell$, link inertia $I_\ell$, and link
holonomy stiffness $K_\ell$, the transverse long-wavelength branch satisfies
\begin{align}
    \omega^2(\mathbf k)
    &=
    c_T^2\lvert\mathbf k\rvert^2
    +
    O(a_\ell^4\lvert\mathbf k\rvert^4),
    \\
    c_T
    &=
    a_\ell\sqrt{\frac{K_\ell}{I_\ell}}.
    \label{eq:genesis_link_speed}
\end{align}

This is the modern action-level descendant of the early oscillator construction
$v\sim \lambda\sqrt{k/m}$. The difference is decisive:
Equation~\eqref{eq:genesis_link_speed} follows from a declared set of degrees of
freedom and an action, while the early formula substituted measured particle
quantities directly into a harmonic-oscillator analogy.

The material Euler-vorticity sector and the link-torsion sector remain distinct:
\begin{align}
    \boldsymbol{\zeta}
    &=
    \nabla\times\mathbf v,
    &
    \boldsymbol{\mathcal B}_\ell
    &=
    \nabla\times\boldsymbol{\mathcal A}_\ell.
    \label{eq:material_vorticity_link_curvature_distinction}
\end{align}
A torsion pulse may therefore propagate by changing relational link states
without creating permanent material vorticity in an initially irrotational
material element.

\subsection{The non-circular route to the propagation speed}
\label{subsec:noncircular_route_to_c}

\textbf{[NON-CIRCULARITY GUARD].}
The empirical value of $c$ may be used in calibrated phenomenology, but it may
not be used directly or indirectly in a derivation that is presented as a
prediction of $c$.

A legitimate derivation has the logical structure
\begin{align}
    \text{microgeometry and action}
    \longrightarrow
    (a_\ell,I_\ell,K_\ell)
    \longrightarrow
    c_T
    \longrightarrow
    c_T\stackrel{?}{=}c_{\mathrm{obs}}.
    \label{eq:noncircular_c_chain}
\end{align}

A circular construction has the structure
\begin{align}
    c_{\mathrm{obs}}
    \longrightarrow
    \text{calibrated microparameter}
    \longrightarrow
    c_T
    \longrightarrow
    c_{\mathrm{obs}}.
    \label{eq:circular_c_chain}
\end{align}

For a candidate dimensional basis $(\Gamma_\star,\rho_\star,r_\star)$, one may
write
\begin{align}
    a_\ell
    &=
    \xi_\ell r_\star,
    \\
    I_\ell
    &=
    \iota_\ell\rho_\star a_\ell^5,
    \\
    K_\ell
    &=
    \kappa_\ell\rho_\star\Gamma_\star^2a_\ell,
    \label{eq:zero_legacy_link_scaling}
\end{align}
which gives
\begin{align}
    c_T
    =
    \frac{\Gamma_\star}{a_\ell}
    \sqrt{\frac{\kappa_\ell}{\iota_\ell}}
    =
    \frac{\Gamma_\star}{r_\star}
    \frac{1}{\xi_\ell}
    \sqrt{\frac{\kappa_\ell}{\iota_\ell}}.
    \label{eq:zero_legacy_c_candidate}
\end{align}

The set $(\Gamma_\star,\rho_\star,r_\star)$ is a candidate dimensional basis,
not yet the asserted ontological core of SST. Its numerical values must remain
symbolic in a zero-legacy derivation unless their provenance is independent of
$c$, $h$, $e$, $m_e$, $\alpha$, $\epsilon_0$, $\mu_0$, and any other validation
target.

\subsection{Three valid modes of use}
\label{subsec:three_valid_modes_of_use}

The same equations may be used under three different epistemic modes:
\begin{description}
    \item[Symbolic mode.] All dimensional primitives remain symbolic. The
    outputs are dimensionless spectra, ratios, topology-dependent coefficients,
    and scaling laws. This is the cleanest theory-development mode.

    \item[Calibrated consistency mode.] Current canonical numerical values may
    be inserted to test stability, dispersion, convergence, mode count, energy
    conservation, and order of magnitude. The result is labelled
    \textbf{[CALIBRATED CONSISTENCY TEST]}, not \textbf{[PREDICTION]}.

    \item[Prediction mode.] A preregistered set of independent observables fixes
    the dimensional scale and any permitted coefficients. All remaining
    observables are then computed without retuning. Only those unused
    observables qualify as predictions.
\end{description}

The use of known numbers in simulation is therefore not concealed. What is
forbidden is to use a known number to define an input and later count its
recovery as an independent success.

\subsection{From relative rotation to operational spacetime}
\label{subsec:relative_rotation_operational_spacetime_genesis}

The notebooks combined Lorentz transformations, moving frames, rotating matter,
and internal clock ideas before the modern Swirl Clock notation existed. The
persistent question was whether the time and length measured by matter could be
properties of its internal recurrent dynamics rather than primitive properties
of a detached coordinate system.

The modern formulation distinguishes substrate ordering from operational
measurement. A stable carrier $A$ constructs proper time $\tau_A$, calibrated
length $\ell_A$, and radar coordinates with exchanged torsion pulses. The local
statement is
\begin{align}
    c_A^{(2\mathrm w)}
    =
    \frac{2\ell_A}{\Delta\tau_A}
    =
    c_T.
    \label{eq:genesis_local_radar_speed}
\end{align}

The target is not merely that clocks slow. The same link-field cone must govern
clock response, rod equilibration, synchronization, and free propagation. Only
then can Alice and Bob reconstruct the same local invariant speed while
assigning different temporal and spatial coordinates to the same event.

The languages of relative vorticity and relativistic congruence kinematics
both use expansion, shear, rotation, and acceleration. This is only a structural
vocabulary, not an identification by analogy. SST must derive the
observer-dependent kinematic decomposition from the actual vortex and link-field
dynamics.

\subsection{Canonical provenance and dependency rules}
\label{subsec:canonical_provenance_dependency_rules}

The following rules govern all claims developed from the origin program:

\begin{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{Single-source rule.}
    A claim is not normatively part of SST merely because it occurs in a chat,
    notebook, old manuscript, code comment, or research-track file. Active
    foundational claims must appear in the MAIN Canon with status and
    dependencies.

    \item \textbf{No-backprojection rule.}
    Modern vortex, gauge, knot, or link-field mathematics must not be described
    as literally present in an early notebook unless the source supports that
    statement.

    \item \textbf{Dependency rule.}
    Every claimed prediction must have an acyclic dependency path from declared
    inputs to output.

    \item \textbf{Calibration-exclusion rule.}
    A quantity used directly or indirectly to determine an input parameter may
    not also be counted as an independent prediction.

    \item \textbf{Multiple-route rule.}
    A proposed structural constant should, where possible, be obtained through
    at least two genuinely independent sectors---for example a link-field
    propagation/constitutive calculation and a bound-mode, static-source, or
    transition-action calculation. Rewriting one capacitance identity in
    impedance notation does not count as a second route.

    \item \textbf{Historical-preservation rule.}
    Incorrect or incomplete old mechanisms are retained in the provenance
    appendix under \textbf{[ORIGIN]} or \textbf{[REJECTED]}, while the corrected
    modern formulation is placed beside them.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Extended epistemic labels}
\label{subsec:extended_epistemic_labels}

The origin and dependency architecture uses the following labels in addition to
the primary Canon stratification:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabularx}{0.94\textwidth}{@{}>{\bfseries}p{0.24\textwidth}X@{}}
\toprule
Label & Meaning \\
\midrule
\texttt{[ORIGIN]} & Historical idea or source statement; not a present derivation. \\
\texttt{[FOUNDATIONAL POSTULATE]} & Explicit starting commitment of the current model. \\
\texttt{[HISTORICAL BRIDGE]} & Algebraic or conceptual link from the old formulation to known physics. \\
\texttt{[DERIVED]} & Consequence of explicitly declared current assumptions. \\
\texttt{[CALIBRATED]} & Numerical quantity fixed from external data or a bridge relation. \\
\texttt{[PREDICTION]} & Output not used directly or indirectly in calibration. \\
\texttt{[BRIDGE]} & Explicit translation from an orthodox structure into an SST candidate structure; not a completed derivation of the SST identification. \\
\texttt{[RESEARCH]} & Active, falsifiable programme whose closure conditions remain open. \\
\texttt{[HYPOTHESIS]} & Specific testable preference or existence claim not yet established. \\
\texttt{[ANSATZ]} & Declared trial form used for calculation or continuation; not selected by the governing equations unless separately derived. \\
\texttt{[THEOREM TARGET]} & Statement that must be obtained from the action or governing equations. \\
\texttt{[FALSIFIER]} & Preregistered result that would reject the stated hypothesis. \\
\texttt{[REJECTED]} & Historical mechanism retained for provenance but not active in the model. \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}

\subsection{Immediate theorem targets}
\label{subsec:genesis_immediate_theorem_targets}

The rediscovered impedance path yields the following immediate research targets:

\begin{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{Link propagation theorem.}
    Derive $I_\ell$, $K_\ell$, and $a_\ell$ from the microscopic adjacency and
    interaction law, and prove the existence of an isotropic transverse branch
    with a controlled infrared and front velocity.

    \item \textbf{Monometricity theorem.}
    Show that all physically relevant torsion polarizations and all stable
    vortex-matter species reconstruct the same local characteristic cone.

    \item \textbf{Transition-action theorem.}
    Derive a minimal transition action $\mathcal J_0$ from a closed phase-space
    cycle or a topological transition, rather than setting
    $\mathcal J_0=h$ or $\hbar$.

    \item \textbf{Source-quantization theorem.}
    Derive the minimal source unit $Q_0$ from knot/link topology or a Noether
    charge, rather than inserting the elementary SI charge.

    \item \textbf{Impedance-ratio theorem.}
    Derive $\kappa_Z$ and test
    Equation~\eqref{eq:alpha_impedance_candidate} without using $\alpha$ in any
    input or normalization.

    \item \textbf{Free--bound conversion theorem.}
    Construct a coupled action for a propagating link excitation and a bound
    vortex mode, and derive absorption, emission, selection rules, linewidth,
    and energy conservation.

    \item \textbf{Clock--rod--cone closure theorem.}
    Derive the response of vortex clocks and rods from the same link-field cone
    that governs propagation, rather than imposing Lorentz kinematics as an
    additional unrelated rule.

    \item \textbf{Resolved-knot certification target.}
    Across independent seeds and both the $N=600$ and $N=1200$ branches,
    demonstrate convergence of topology, thickness, residual,
    smooth-upper-bound, and contact measures for the selected bound-state
    geometry.

    \item \textbf{KAM-caged knot-state target.}
    For a vortex ring and then a certified trefoil, find a converged relative
    equilibrium or periodic relative orbit, remove symmetry and gauge modes,
    establish reduced Floquet ellipticity, extract a nonlinear twist matrix,
    and test whether a positive-measure family of invariant tori survives the
    full finite-core Biot--Savart correction.

    \item \textbf{Bound-mode impedance target.}
    Derive the frequency-dependent transition response on a certified finite-
    thickness knot domain and show which contact-skeleton observables enter
    $Z_{\mathrm J}(\omega)$; scalar ropelength alone is not an admissible closure.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Preregistered falsifiers and integrity failures}
\label{subsec:genesis_preregistered_falsifiers}

The impedance and non-circularity programme uses four distinct failure classes.
They must not be conflated: a methodological failure voids a derivation claim,
whereas an empirical failure conflicts directly with observation.

\paragraph{Class E --- empirical falsifiers.}
\begin{enumerate}
    \item the inferred propagation speed depends irreducibly on polarization,
    lattice orientation, or vortex species at low energy;
    \item clock slowing, rod response, synchronization, and propagation require
    mutually inconsistent characteristic speeds.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Class T --- theoretical or model-closure falsifiers.}
\begin{enumerate}
    \item no stable transverse link branch exists;
    \item the transition action or source unit remains continuously tunable
    rather than dynamically or topologically quantized;
    \item a free torsion pulse necessarily creates permanent material vorticity
    in an initially irrotational region, contrary to the declared material--link
    sector separation.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Class N --- numerical or certification failures.}
\begin{enumerate}
    \item the selected knot geometry changes topology, component linking data,
    or strict near-ideal classification under the declared refinement pipeline;
    \item scalar ropelength converges while the positive strut/kink contact
    measures or active rigidity rank fail to converge;
    \item a claimed KAM-caged state loses reduced Floquet ellipticity, nonlinear
    twist, invariant-torus measure, or bounded action drift under refinement.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Class M --- methodological-integrity failures.}
\begin{enumerate}
    \item the value of $c_T$ can match experiment only by inserting a parameter
    whose provenance already contains $c$;
    \item the impedance-ratio route reproduces $\alpha$ only after
    $\alpha$-dependent calibration;
    \item alternative microparameters fit $c$ and $\alpha$ equally well while
    making incompatible predictions elsewhere, without a dynamical selection
    principle;
    \item a downstream mass, impedance, or constant extraction quotes precision
    parametrically smaller than the certified geometry-error envelope without an
    independent cancellation proof;
    \item a preregistered gate or tolerance is weakened after inspecting the
    downstream physical output.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{[CANON INTERPRETATION].}
A Class~M event does not by itself falsify the physical hypothesis; it voids the
claimed derivation and returns the result to calibrated-consistency status.

\subsection{Canonical dependency summary}
\label{subsec:canonical_dependency_summary}

The intended non-circular dependency graph is
\begin{align}
    &\text{micro-adjacency, symmetry, topology, and local action}
    \nonumber\\
    &\quad\longrightarrow
    \{a_\ell,I_\ell,K_\ell,Q_0,\mathcal J_0,\kappa_Z\}
    \nonumber\\
    &\quad\longrightarrow
    \{c_T,Z_\ell,Z_{\mathrm J},\alpha_{\mathrm{SST}}\}
    \nonumber\\
    &\quad\longrightarrow
    \text{clock, rod, spectral, atomic, and scattering observables}
    \nonumber\\
    &\quad\longrightarrow
    \text{comparison with }\{c,h,e,\alpha,m_e,\ldots\}_{\mathrm{obs}}.
    \label{eq:canonical_zero_legacy_dependency_graph}
\end{align}

The reverse arrows are permitted only in the explicitly calibrated
phenomenology layer. They are prohibited in a claimed zero-legacy prediction.

\subsection{Cross-document operating rule}
\label{subsec:genesis_cross_document_operating_rule}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{[CANON OPERATING RULE].}
Every SST sub-paper, software benchmark, and numerical data release shall state
for each material result whether it is used in \texttt{symbolic},
\texttt{calibrated-consistency}, or \texttt{prediction} mode, and shall attach an
explicit epistemic label such as \textbf{[ORTHODOX]}, \textbf{[DERIVED]},
\textbf{[RESEARCH TRACK]}, or \textbf{[SPECULATIVE]}.  A result may be promoted
only after its dependency graph, gate profile, and error envelope are exposed.
\end{quote}
